金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 9 月 30 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國企業投資動能維持韌性 ：8 月標普全球美國綜合 PMI 升至 58.4，創2021 年 7 月來高點 ，
製造業、服務業同步擴張至 57 和 58.7； 8 月扣除飛機與國防的核心資本財訂單月增 1.6%，
高於前月修正後的 0.6%。其中，初級金屬、機械及電機設備訂單均呈現成長，顯示企業資本
支出、AI 投資與國防需求仍具韌性，持續支撐美國經濟成長。
◼ 美中同意貿易休兵再延長兩個月：美中同意調降雙邊總值 600 億美元商品的關稅，涵蓋美國

玉米、化妝品及中國家電、玩具等產品，並延長貿易休兵至明年 1 月 10 日，同時中國承諾繼
續供應稀土並擴大採購美國農產品與煤礦，美國則繼續暫停實施 50%穿透性規則；AI 方面，
美中雙方雖未就 AI 監管議題達成共識 ， 但同意建立風險溝通機制 ， 並於11 月底前展開對話 。
◼ Meta 旗下 AI 代理人 Muse 登頂排行榜：Meta 新款個人 AI 代理 Muse 上線後迅速累積逾
250 萬次下載，並登上美國 App Store 下載排行榜首位。相較過去生成式 AI 以問答與內容生
成為主，Muse 可進一步協助安排日程、搜尋資訊、處理文件及完成交易，代表 AI 正從提升
效率工具逐步走向數位勞動力，市場評價焦點也將逐漸由 AI 基礎設施擴散至應用軟體業者。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，科技供應鏈成長動能延續：AI 基礎建設需求持續擴張，帶動 HBM、伺服器
DRAM、 先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持成長動能 ， 市場表現由全面上漲逐步轉向結構性分
化。若 AI 伺服器需求持續成長且 CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優勢、定價能力及較高訂單

能見度之企業，其營運表現有機會獲得支撐。整體而言，科技產業獲利表現仍具關注度，惟後
續仍須留意全球景氣、產業供需變化、政策監管及市場評價調整等風險因素。
◼ 債券佈局可關注中天期美國公債及投等債：中天期美國公債及投資級債仍值得持續關注：**雖
然貨幣政策風險下降有助於穩定長期通膨預期 ， 但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應 ，
以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，
中天期債券於票息收益與存續期間風險之間相對較為均衡。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公
司債指數利差約 80 個基點，雖然利差偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供相對較高的收益水準。
因此，在目前市場環境下，中天期美國公債及投資級債仍為值得持續觀察的債券資產類別，惟
後續表現仍須留意利率走勢、信用利差變化及經濟成長動能等因素。

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國經濟成長放緩但仍具韌性，通膨居高，消費信心轉弱，Fed 維持鷹派立場 
經濟 
⚫ 2026 年第 2 季美國實質 GDP 季增年率 1.5%，低於第 1 季 2.1%，顯示
經濟成長略趨緩。消費支出貢獻 2.31 個百分點為主要動能，惟淨出口、
庫存調整及政府支出轉弱形成拖累；內需仍具韌性，但通膨壓力尚未明
顯緩解。 
⚫ 8 月密西根大學消費者信心指數由 55.2 降至 51.7，月減 6.3%、年減
11.2%，現況與預期指數同步下滑。調查指出，高物價、汽油價格預期
及政策不確定性壓抑信心；一年通膨預期由 4.2%降至 4.0%，長期預期
持平 3.3%。 
⚫ 7 月美國新屋銷售年化率 60.7 萬套，較上月及去年同期分別減少 10.5%
及 6.3%；房價中位數續跌，供給月數升至 9.6 個月，反映銷售動能降
溫，庫存去化速度放緩，整體市場供給仍維持相對充裕。 
⚫ 7 月美國 PCE 物價指數月增 0.2%，由前月-0.1%回升，年增率持平於
3.7%；核心 PCE 月增 0.2%、年增 3.3%，顯示通膨壓力仍具黏著性。另
一方面，個人所得及可支配所得分別月增 0.4%及 0.5%，惟實質消費支
出由前月成長 0.4%放緩至持平，儲蓄率則升至 3.0%，顯示內需動能略
為降溫。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 美加貿易爭端持續升溫 ， 美國自8 月 22 日起對加拿大部分商品加徵 50%
關稅；加拿大則公布涵蓋約700 項、總值276 億美元美國商品的報復性
關稅措施，將於 9 月 8 日生效。此外，川普表示擬於 2027 年將加拿大
汽車及鋼鐵關稅提高至 50%。 整體而言 ， 美加已進入實質貿易報復階段 ，
恐增加北美供應鏈與經貿不確定性。 
⚫ 川普於 8 月 26 日簽署第 14420 號行政命令，針對外國製大規模電力系
統設備可能帶來的資安漏洞 、 遠端操控及供應鏈中斷等國安風險宣布國
家緊急狀態，並授權能源部對高風險設備的採購、進口、轉讓及安裝進
行審查、限制或附加條件管理，以強化美國電網安全與供應鏈韌性；能
源部須於 120 天內完成相關執行規則制定。 
⚫ Fed 主席華許於 Jackson Hole 首場演說釋出偏鷹訊號，指美國通膨仍高
於 Fed 2%目標， 若未明確回落， 仍保留升息空間； 同時主張淡化前瞻指

東京通膨升溫，潛在物價壓力持續增強

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.8.31 
一、 美國—經濟成長放緩但仍具韌性，通膨居高，消費信心轉弱，Fed 維持鷹
派立場 
美國商務部經濟分析局(BEA)公布 2026 年第 2 季 GDP 第二次估值，美國第
2 季實質 GDP 季增年率為 1.5%，與初估值相同，但低於第 1 季的 2.1%，顯示經
濟維持溫和擴張但成長動能略為放緩。個人消費支出為主要成長動能，貢獻 2.31
個百分點，其中服務及商品消費分別貢獻 1.42 及 0.89 個百分點，反映民間需求
仍具韌性；私人投資則貢獻0.48 個百分點，其中設備投資及智慧財產產品投資表
現穩健，但受庫存調整拖累部分成長。另一方面，淨出口拖累經濟成長1.14 個百
分點，主因進口大幅增加抵銷出口成長效益；政府消費支出及投資亦負貢獻 0.16
個百分點。值得注意的是，反映內需基本面的實質私人國內最終銷售成長4.2%，
顯示民間需求仍具支撐力。整體而言，第 2 季經濟成長主要由消費及固定投資帶
動 ， 但受進口增加 、 庫存調整及政府支出下滑影響 ， 成長速度較前季放緩 ； 此外 ，
PCE 物價指數及核心 PCE 物價指數分別上升 5.3%及 3.6%，顯示通膨壓力仍偏
高，後續仍須關注消費、投資及通膨發展對經濟前景之影響。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 8 月美國消費者信心指數終值降至 51.7，低於 7 月
的 55.2，月減 6.3%、年減 11.2%，顯示民眾信心在 7 月回升後再度轉弱；其中，
當前經濟狀況指數由 54.8 降至 51.9，月減 5.3%、年減 15.9%，消費者預期指數亦
由 55.4 降至 51.5，月減 7.0%、年減 7.9%，反映民眾對現況與未來景氣看法同步
惡化。調查指出，8 月信心下滑涵蓋各政治族群，其中共和黨人降幅尤為明顯，
且高齡、低中所得及未持股等較難承受生活成本上升的消費者信心降幅較大；在
包含伊朗衝突在內的政策不確定性下，消費者預期汽油價格短期及長期仍將上升，
並更加擔憂經濟其他領域前景轉弱， 未來一年與五年商業環境預期分別下滑10%
及 13%。通膨預期方面，未來一年通膨預期由 7 月 4.2%降至 4.0%，但仍高於伊
朗衝突前 2 月的 3.4%及所有 2024 年讀數；長期通膨預期則連續第三個月持平於
3.3%，仍略高於 2024 年 2.8%至 3.2%的區間。整體而言，8 月美國消費信心再度
降溫，顯示高物價、汽油價格預期與政策不確定性仍壓抑民眾信心，且若貿易緊
張再升級，可能進一步加劇消費者悲觀情緒。

2026 年 7 月美國新屋銷售經季節性調整年化率為 60.7 萬套，較上月減少
10.5%，亦較去年同期衰退 6.3%；地區銷售表現分歧，其中東北部及西部分別較
上月增加 30.3%及 6.2%，中西部及南部則分別減少 42.7%及 13.0%。價格方面，
7 月新屋銷售中位價為 39.38 萬美元，月減 2.3%、年減 0.9%。供給方面，7 月底
經季節性調整後待售新屋數量為 48.8 萬套，較 6 月底增加 1.9%，但較去年同期
減少 1.6%；按目前銷售速度估算，供給月數由 6 月的 8.5 個月升至 9.6 個月，亦
高於去年同期的 9.2 個月 。 整體而言 ，7 月美國新屋銷售較上月及去年同期下滑 ，
中位房價續降，庫存及供給月數則有所增加，顯示在銷售放緩下，市場庫存去化
速度趨緩，整體供給仍維持相對充裕水準。 
2026 年 7 月美國個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數月增率由 6 月的-0.1%回升至
0.2%，年增率則持平於 3.7%；扣除食品與能源後之核心 PCE 月增率由 0.1%升至
0.2%，年增率亦維持在 3.3%，顯示整體通膨壓力雖未進一步升高，但月度物價漲
勢較前月略有回溫。另一方面，個人所得及可支配所得分別月增 0.4%及 0.5%，
均高於上月表現 ， 實質可支配所得亦成長0.4%； 惟實質個人消費支出由上月月增
0.4%放緩至持平 ， 顯示民間消費動能略有降溫 。 在所得增幅高於消費增幅帶動下 ，
個人儲蓄率由 2.6%升至 3.0%。整體而言，7 月美國通膨表現大致持穩、所得持續
增長且儲蓄意願提升，但消費支出趨於平緩，顯示內需動能較前月略為放緩，後
續仍須持續關注通膨走勢、消費表現及其對貨幣政策之影響。 
美加貿易爭端於 8 月持續升溫 ， 在美加貿易談判於8 月 21 日破局後 ， 美國依
《貿易法》第 338 條對加拿大汽車、酒類、乳製品、家具等產品加徵 50%關稅措
施已於 8 月 22 日正式生效，加拿大總理卡尼(Mark Carney) 則表示將採取對等反
制措施。8 月 25 日，加拿大財政部公布完整報復性關稅清單，涵蓋約700 項美國
商品、總值約 276 億美元，依照美國關稅稅率對應課徵 15%、25%及 50%不等的
反制關稅，其中鋼鋁產品稅率提高至 50%，部分家電、乳製品及鋼鋁衍生產品課
徵 25%，電機設備及工具則課徵 15%，預計於 9 月 8 日正式生效，並同步推出數
十億美元規模的勞工及企業支援方案。此外，川普總統於 8 月 24 日透過社群媒
體表示，擬自 2027 年 1 月 1 日起將加拿大汽車、卡車、汽車零組件及鋼鐵產品
關稅由現行 25%提高至 50%，惟截至目前尚未發布正式行政命令。整體而言，美
加關稅衝突已由雙邊談判擴大至實質貿易報復階段，未來若相關措施全面落實，
恐進一步增加北美供應鏈成本與區域經貿不確定性。

### Page 7

用相關內容。

6

美國總統川普於 8 月 26 日簽署第 14420 號行政命令 ， 依據 《國際緊急經濟權
力法》 （IEEPA） 及 《國家緊急狀態法》 （NEA） ， 就外國供應大型電力系統 （Bulk-
Power System ）設備可能衍生的資安漏洞、遠端存取、惡意操作、蓄意破壞及供
應鏈中斷風險宣布國家緊急狀態。該命令授權美國能源部對涉及國家安全風險的
外國製電力設備之採購 、 進口 、轉讓及安裝實施限制或審查 ，適用範圍涵蓋69 千
伏以上輸電系統，以及維持電力系統可靠運作所需的發電與控制設施，包括變壓
器、逆變器、儲能系統、發電與工業控制設備，以及相關軟體、韌體與遠端維護
服務，但不包括地方配電設施。行政命令要求能源部於 120 天內制定執行規則，
並於 180 天內提出聯邦採購規範修正建議，以降低外國供應鏈風險、強化美國電
網安全與關鍵基礎設施韌性，並使聯邦能源基礎設施採購優先考量國家安全與美
國製造設備。 
Fed 新任主席華許(Kevin Warsh)於 8 月 28 日在 Jackson Hole 全球央行年會發
表上任後首場主題演說，釋出偏鷹派訊號。他表示，美國通膨雖已自高點回落，
但 7 月 PCE 年增率仍達 3.7%，明顯高於聯準會(Fed)2%目標，近期通膨數據改善
仍不足以證明潛在通膨趨勢已實質好轉；若決策官員無法確認通膨正以明確且足
夠的速度回落至目標水準，Fed「仍有工作要做」 ，市場因而解讀為未來仍保留進
一步升息空間。華許同時主張淡化前瞻指引(Forward Guidance)，降低市場對利率
預測與點陣圖的依賴，打造「更安靜的聯準會」，並強調貨幣政策應保有彈性，
依據即時經濟數據、趨勢及金融情勢作出判斷。在人工智慧 (AI)方面，華許認為
AI 可能成為新的生產要素，有助推升長期生產力與潛在成長率，但其對通膨、就
業市場及貨幣政策的影響仍有待觀察。 
二、歐元區—經濟增溫，歐洲央行 9 月升息預期增加 
 歐元區 7 月廣義貨幣供給(M3)年增率增加 0.1 個百分點至 3.4%，長天期存款
(M3-M2)年增率則降 0.2 個百分點至 4.2%；另外，家庭貸款年增率增加 0.1 個百
分點至 3%、非金融企業貸款餘額增加 0.4 個百分點至 4.4%。整體而言，家庭與
企業融資需求持續擴張，加上經濟表現優於預期，帶動貨幣供給穩步增長，亦為
歐洲央行 9 月進一步升息提供政策空間。 
歐元區 8 月經濟景氣指數增加 1.3 點至 98.4，反映消費者與企業信心同步改
善。其中，零售業及服務業信心分別上升 0.7 點與 1.1 點，顯示內需活動與服務

需求有所回溫 ； 此外 ， 就業預期指數增加1.5 點至 98.9， 企業聘僱計畫轉趨積極 ，

由於中國廉價商品大量湧入歐盟，對歐洲製造業形成壓力，歐盟對中國的貿
易逆差已接近每日 10 億歐元，因此歐盟委員會主席馮德萊恩在 8 月 27 日表示，
若歐中貿易談判未能有效改善雙方貿易失衡問題，歐盟不排除進一步運用反脅迫
機制及貿易防衛工具。此舉反映歐盟對中國產能過剩、補貼政策及關鍵供應鏈依
賴的疑慮持續升高，未來雙方貿易摩擦恐有進一步升溫風險。 
三、日本—東京通膨升溫，潛在物價壓力持續增強 
8 月東京核心通膨增加 0.1 個百分點至 1.8%，雖然政府透過電力、天然氣補
貼及免費托兒措施抑制部分物價漲幅，但扣除相關政策因素後，核心通膨（扣除
生鮮食品）及核心核心通膨（扣除生鮮食品及能源）均已高於日本央行 2%的政
策目標。從細項來看，家具、家用品等商品價格持續攀升；食品價格雖因米價回
落而略有緩和，但外食價格年增 5.0%，反映食品相關價格壓力尚未明顯消退；醫
療服務及租金漲幅均超過 2%，顯示通膨壓力已由商品擴散至服務領域。整體而
言 ， 潛在通膨動能持續增強 ， 若未來政府補貼措施逐步退場 ， 通膨仍有上行風險 ，
將提高央行緊縮貨幣政策的壓力。 
四、中國—歐美對中國貿易圍堵壓力持續增加 
1-7 月工業部門營業收入、利潤年增率分別為 6.5%、17.6%，其中營業收入較
上期(1-6 月)持平，利潤增速則降 1.1 個百分點。受惠於金屬、塑化原物料上漲，
加上 AI 需求擴張，以及政府推動高科技產業發展等因素，營收成長貢獻度較高
者包含有色金屬加工(營收、利潤年增 19.6%、91.8%)、計算機、通信和其他電子
設備製造業(營收 、 利潤年增19.4%、105%)、 化學原料及製品(營收 、 利潤年增9.3%、
56.6%)。此外，電氣機械及器材業、汽車業營收年增率雖分別為 10%、2.7%，但
獲利分別年減 7.6%、20.4%，顯示產業陷入價格競爭壓力，使利潤受到擠壓。非
金屬礦物製品業營收、獲利下滑13.7、48.2%，反映產能過剩情形嚴重；黑色金屬
加工業營收雖下滑 6.4%，惟獲利止跌回升 2%，反映鋼價暫時回升。整體而言，
部分產業受惠於短期原物料價格波動，以及受到政策推動的高科技產業表現較
佳，惟其他多數產業營收獲利表現較為平淡，產業分化趨勢明顯。

展望未來， 歐盟近期已針對從中國進口的電池 、 輪胎 、 紡織原料等展開調查 ，

可能進一步提高貿易防衛措施。此外，美國財政部長貝森特於 8 月 31 日公開呼
籲 G20 國家對中國築起貿易壁壘 ， 亦可能會施壓人民幣升值以緩解全球貿易失衡
情形。在歐美同步關注中國龐大貿易順差的情況下，中國貿易環境面臨的阻力恐
持續增加，亦不利於工業部門發展。 
五、印度及東南亞—泰國、菲律賓央行政策分歧，7 月貿易續呈逆差 
(1) 新加坡：7 月消費者物價指數年增率升至 2.2%，為 2024 年 8 月以來的最高，
其中食品 （2.2%） 、住房和水電 （1.3%） 、 交通 （7.9%） 以及醫療保健 （3.3%）
的價格漲幅加快 ； 剔除住宿和私人交通成本的核心通膨率從1.6%上升至 2%，
整體而言 ， 通膨上揚主要受公用事業價格上漲的推動 ， 商品和服務價格仍保持
穩定。 
(2) 泰國：泰國央行 8 月會議維持政策利率於 1%不變，保持寬鬆的貨幣政策以支
持經濟復甦 ， 並強調金融機構應增加對弱勢群體和潛在中小企業的支持性 。 央
行表示，經濟成長依然緩慢且不均衡，由於生活成本上升，家庭保持謹慎，私
人消費疲軟 ， 但受強於預期的商品出口和與科技及人工智慧週期相關的私人投
資所支撐，預計 2026 年及 2027 年經濟成長為 2.3%、1.8%；通膨方面，受聖
嬰現象及能源價格上漲傳導效應，預估至 2027 年第一季前將上揚，隨後將因
疲弱的國內需求及較低的潛在經濟成長而下滑。 
7 月出口額年增 21.6%(348 億美元)， 主要由工業產品出口成長24.2%所帶動 ，
其中電腦及相關設備（63.2%） 、電話及零件 （160.3%）及電路板 （35.2%）增
幅最為顯著 ， 對美國出口年增45.3%、 對中國和日本分別成長15.2%、21.3%。
7 月進口額年增 36.7%(384 億美元)，其中燃料、原材料和中間產品、資本貨物
進口成長達均逾 40%以上。進出口相抵，7 月貿易逆差達 36.1 億美元，為連續
第九個月出現逆差。 
(3) 菲律賓:菲律賓央行 8 月會議升息 1 碼至 5.0%，旨在抑制持續的通膨壓力。央
行表示，儘管通膨有所緩和，但油價波動、聖嬰現象對農業的影響及潛在的工
資調整仍構成上行風險 ， 預計2026 年和 2027 年平均整體通膨率仍將突破 4.0%
的容忍上限，2028 年才會回落至 3.0%的目標水準。不過，央行對經濟成長前

景保持樂觀態度 ，預計隨著財政支出加速和年底預算執行力度加大 ，經濟成長

消。穆迪同時預期，菲律賓未來兩年財政指標，將因經濟成長逐漸復甦及政府
對財政整頓的持續承諾而維持穩定。 
六、台灣—景氣對策信號連八月呈紅燈 
7 月景氣對策信號續呈紅燈，綜合判斷分數為 41 分，與上月持平，其中製造

M1B 受外資淨匯出等影響，由黃紅燈轉呈綠燈，其他七項燈號維持不變。另領先
與同時指標皆呈上升，顯示國內經濟動能維持穩健。

1 群益投顧 
ASML (ASML US) 與台積電 (2330 TT) 共同推動 High NA EUV 將自現行的 6 吋光罩，
轉移至更大的 12 吋 光 罩。 目標於 2031 年之前建立 12 吋 光 罩 試 產 線(pilot line) ， 並 於
2033 年之前實現微影系統全面就緒。台積電並表示自 2030 年 起 ， 將 在 先 進 製 程 量 產
中導入 ASML 的 High NA  EUV 。台積電與 ASML 共同發起 6 吋光罩轉移至 12 吋光
罩 的 規 格 轉 移 ， 其 戰 略 意 義 在 於 ， 製 訂 規 格 和 標 準 ， 確 保 台 積 電 技 術 領 先 地 位 。 因 此
對 台 積 電 的 投 資 評 等 Buy ，目標價 3,200 元。

研 究 員 ： 陳 俐 妍   
ly.c@capital.com.tw

ASML 與 台 積 電 共 同 推 動 High NA EUV 將 自 現 行 的 6 吋 光 罩 ， 轉 移 至 更 大
的 12 吋 光 罩 ：  
09/07/2026 ASML 與 台 積 電 於 BACUS 國 際 光 學 工 程 學 會(SPIE) 先 進 微 影 技
術 研 討 會 前 宣 布 發 起 產 業 合 作 ， 共 同 推 動High NA EUV 將 自 現 行 的 6 吋光
罩 ， 轉 移 至 更 大 的 12 吋 光 罩 ， 預 期 將 進 一 步 提 升 晶 圓 廠 的 生 產 力 、 降 低 晶
片 製 造 成 本 ， 並 消 除 需 要 2 張 6 吋 光 罩 在 High NA EUV 曝 光 時 進 行 拼 接 的
限 制，使 整 個 半 導 體 產 業 受 惠。此 轉 移 能 讓 先 進 晶 片 製 造 服 務 提 供 者 充 分 利
用 High NA EUV 的 優 勢 ， 更 提 升 未 來 世 代 先 進 晶 片 的 成 本 效 益 。 
目標於 2031 年 之 前 建 立 12 吋 光 罩 試 產 線(pilot line) ，並於 2033 年 之 前 實
現 微 影 系 統 全 面 就 緒 ， 以 支 援 先 進 製 程 量 產 。 台 積 電 並 表 示 自2030 年 起 ，
將 在 先 進 製 程 量 產 中 導 入 ASML 的 High NA 技 術 。  
12 吋光罩 使 High NA 效 率 提 升 ：  
現行 EUV 設備的 NA(Numerical Aperture ， NA) 為 0.33 ， High NA 則 提 高 到
0.55 。 NA 數 值 越 高 ， 解 析 度 也 越 高 、 曝 光 時 能 呈 現 的 細 節 愈 多 。ASML 資
料顯示 High NA 可 將 最 小 解 析 尺 寸 從 現 行 EUV 的約 13 奈 米 進 一 步 縮 小 到
8 奈 米 ， 也 就 是 能 在 晶 圓 上 印 出 更 細 微 的 電 路 圖 形 。 部 分 原 本 需 要2 次 甚 至
更多次曝光才能完成的圖形，也有機會改以單次曝光處理，降低製程複雜
度 、 缺 陷 風 險 與 生 產 周 期 。 
舉 例 來 說 ，0.33 NA EUV 機 台 ， 至 少 需 要 4 張 光 罩 、 分 多 次 曝 光 才 能 完 成
A14 、 A10 金 屬 層 圖 形 ；High NA EUV ， 只 要 1 張 光 罩 就 能 完 成 相 同 圖 形 。
但 解 析 度 提 高 ，High NA 的 光 學 設 計 讓 單 次 可 曝 光 範 圍 縮 小 到 原 本EUV 的
50% ， 製 作 大 顆 晶 片 時 可 能 必 須 分 成2 次 曝 光 ， 再 把 圖 形 拼 接 起 來 。 這 也 是
台積電與 ASML 此 次 推 動 12 吋 光 罩 的 原 因 ， 相 較 現 行 6 吋 光 罩 ， 放 大 光 罩
尺 寸 預 期 能 進 一 步 提 升 晶 圓 廠 生 產 力、降 低 晶 片 製 造 成 本，並 消 除 High NA
曝 光 時 的 拼 接 限 制 。 
台 積 電 預 計 2030 年 開 始 使 用 High NA  EUV ， 推估 High NA 將 會 在 A10 或
A10 以 下 的 先 進 製 程 採 用：  
台積電預計 2030 年 開 始 導入 High NA  EUV ，比 12 吋 光 罩 微 影 系 統 預 計 全
面就緒的 2033 年 更 早 。 這 代 表 台 積 電 初 期 導 入High NA 時 ， 仍 會 先 使 用 現
行 6 吋 光 罩 ， 之 後 再 隨 12 吋 光 罩 與 相 關 系 統 成 熟 逐 步 轉 換 。

### Page 2

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 9 月 9 日

2 群益投顧 
根 據 台 積 電 目 前 公 開 的 製 程 藍 圖 ，A13 與 A12 製 程 都 預 計 2029 年 量 產 。 其
中 A13 是 A14 平 台 的 延 伸 ，A12 則 將 超 級 電 軌(Super Power Rail) 背面供電
技術導入 A14 平 台 。 因 此 N2 、 A16 、 A14 、 A13 與 A12 都 早 於 台 積 電 預 計
2030 年 導 入 High NA ， 推 估 High NA 將 會 在 A10 或 A10 以 下 的 先 進 製 程 採
用。  
Intel (INTC US) 和 Samsung (005930 KS) 領 先 台 積 電 採 用 High NA EUV ，但
也 同 時 參 與 12 吋 光 罩 開 發 ：  
Intel 在 2024 年 宣 布 完 成 全 球 首 套 商 用 High NA EUV 設 備 的 組 裝 ；2026 年
High NA 已 經 正 式 進 入 量 產 ， 並 用 於 部 分 18A 製 程 層 。 是 目 前 High NA 導
入 進 度 最 快 的 晶 片 製 造 商 。Samsung 則預計 2028 年導入 DRAM 量 產 ； 台 積
電選擇在 2030 年 導 入 。Intel 和 Samsung 也 同 時 表 示 與 ASML 及 供 應 鏈 合
作 ， 推 動 未 來 轉 向 大 尺 寸 光 罩 。 
台 積 電 與 ASML 共 同 發 起 6 吋 光 罩 轉 移 至 12 吋 光 罩 的 規 格 轉 移，將 領 導 業
界 規 格 ：  
6 吋 光 罩 轉 移 至 12 吋光罩 的 這 項 升 級 非 單 純 的 尺 寸 放 大 ， 而 是 面 臨 生 態 系
與 物 理 極 限 的 多 重 挑 戰 。 整 體 供 應 鏈 重 構 ， 包 括 基 板 與 材 料 以 及 設 備 等 等 。
12 吋 光 罩 石 英 基 板 的 平 坦 度 、 缺 陷 密 度 與 熱 膨 脹 係 數 控 制 難 度 倍 增 。 製 程
設 備 如 薄 膜 沉 積、蝕 刻、清 洗 與 自 動 化 搬 運 設 備 皆 需 重 新 開 發。後 續 檢 測 與
量測極限檢測難度也增加，更大面積的圖形缺陷檢測（ Inspection ）與修補
技 術 需 要 突 破 。 在 12 吋 面 積 上 維 持 奈 米 級 的 對 位 精 度 與 線 寬 均 勻 性 極 為 困
難   
電 子 設 計 自 動 化 (EDA) 工 具 必 須 重 新 適 應 大 尺 寸 曝 光 視 場 與 資 料 處 理 。 12 
吋 光 罩 的 圖 形 檔 案 與 運 算 複 雜 度 將 大 幅 上 升 。 
台積電與 ASML 共 同 發 起 6 吋 光 罩 轉 移 至 12 吋 光 罩 的 規 格 轉 移，其 戰略意
義在於 ，台 積 電 為 全 球 最 大 晶 圓 代 工 廠，為 先 進 製 程 最 大 的 供 應 商，可 以 領
導 光 罩 基 底 供 應 商 、 光 罩 檢 測 設 備 商， 甚 至 EDA 廠 等 整 個 生 態 系 ，除 共同
分攤 ， 跨 越 升 級 12 吋 光 罩 的 龐 大 研 發 成 本外 ， 製 訂 規 格 和 標 準 ， 確 保 台 積
電 技 術 領 先 地 位 。因 此 對 台 積 電 的投 資 評 等 Buy ， 目 標 價 3,200 元。

3 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Largan Precision  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 Results Miss on Lower
GPM, but FAU/CPO
Development Well on Track
Largan reported 3Q26 net income of NT$5.182bn, down 27% YoY.
The company reported a 11% YoY revenue decline, to NT$15.651bn; gross margin
declined YoY and QoQ to 42.2% in 3Q26, lower than expected due to lower new-
project yields and structural green electricity cost hikes.
Management expects shipments to be flat MoM in October and decline

sequentially in November as order momentum is weaker-than-expected.
FAU/CPO development well on track: First FA  project passed customer
verification; expanding from FA into FAU, including coupling and potential FAU-to-
carrier assembly, with a dedicated new facility expected to support mass production
from around mid-2027. We maintain our long-term positive view on Largan's FAU/
CPO opportunities.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Largan Precision (3008.TW)
Base case: Our price target is based on an SOTP valuation, comprising the traditional business
and potential FAU opportunity. We value the traditional business using a residual income
model, consistent with our Greater China Hardware Tech coverage, assuming an 8.5% cost of
equity, a 7% net profit CAGR over 2026-36E and a 3% terminal growth rate. We separately

value the FAU business, assuming a 40% market share in 2028.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected high-end smartphone demand
n Faster-than-expected penetration of periscopes
n Stronger-than-expected CPO volume growth
Risks to Downside
n Greater-than-expected competition, which would erode Largan's ASP and gross margin
n Weaker-than-expected smartphone demand, especially for high-end models
n Market share gains and more pricing pressure from Sunny

n Weaker-than-expected CPO volume growth

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場或個股之相關
條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保證。非經本公司同意，不得
將本報告加以引用或轉載。 
1 
6667 信紘科 (TRUSVAL)

報告日期： 2026/10/05            研究員：王蕎程 (Joe Wang) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要: 
 訂單滿載與高能見度：信紘科現有訂單達 230 億元，受惠於
AI 與半導體擴建潮，廠務設備與二次配工程訂單滿載，能見度
看至 2028 年。  
 大型統包與海外擴張：業務從單一工程升級至數十億級大型機
電統包，並結合策略夥伴進軍美、日、德、新等海外市場，帶
動營收成長。  
 綠色永續與持續收益：成立專責子公司銳源環境科技，聚焦廢
液處理與資源循環，將一次性工程建置延伸至維修與代操等持
續性收入。

一. 高科技建廠需求強勁，1H26 稅後 EPS 達 7.70 元 
信紘科以特氣特化廠務供應設備系統與二次配裝機工程為主要業
務，近年營運重心與服務範疇持續升級至二次配統包以及機電統
包工程。2025 年高科技產業營收達 59.45 億元，占整體營收高達 
93.56%；2026 年第二季高科技產業營收仍占絕大多數，半導體與
高科技建廠已成為公司最核心且穩定的營收來源

2026 年上半年營收達 32.19 億元；營業毛利 7.49 億元， 毛利率 
23.25% ；營業利益 3.55 億元，稅後淨利 3.79 億元，累計 EPS 達 
7.70 元。目前公司現有在手訂單高達 230 億元，訂 單能見度已直
達 2028 年，整體營運維持強勁成長動能。

二.半導體擴建為主要成長動能，工程與統包範疇擴大 
在廠務與工程服務部分，信紘科目前量產與工程實績包含特氣特
化廠務供應設備、系統建置及二次配管線裝機工程。為進一步提
升單案規模與營運效能，公司將服務領域延伸至二次配統包與大
型機電統包（GC/EPC），使專案金額邁入數十億級規模。後續業
務擴張重點，亦包含綠色製造與循環經濟，於 2025 年 12 月成
立專責子公司「銳源環境科技」，聚焦廢液處理與資源循環，將
一次性建置工程延伸至維修、營運代操及再生產品銷售等多元商
業模式等長期持續性收入。

目前主要服務據點皆配合客戶全球擴產需求建置，公司在台灣擁
有苗栗、台中、台南等據點，憑藉完整的本土供應鏈與人力調度，
能就近支援各大晶圓廠新建、擴建與改建之即時需求（主系統工

三. 預估 2026 / 2027 年稅後 EPS 14.76 / 16.83 元 
預估 2026 全年營收 71.17 億元，YoY +12.0%；營業毛利 16.41

半導體廠與先進封裝建廠需求熱、在手 230 億元訂單高能見度，
加上大型 GC 統包工程持續認列與海外業務擴張，預計 2027 年
營收 78.29 億元，YoY +10.0%，營業毛利 18.18 億元，毛利率 
23.22% ，營業利益 9.76 億元，稅後純益 8.29 億元，稅後 EPS 為 
16.83 元。

四. 投資建議為「買進」，目標價 330 元 
綜合考量 (1)先進製程相關半導體需求維持強勁 (2) 公司可參與
的工程範圍擴大至海外及機電統包等大型工程，並觀察歷史及同
業本益比區間 ， 目前本益比 16 倍評價偏低 ， 故投資建議為「買進」，
目標價 330 元（20x 2027EPS）。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
信紘科技（Trusval Technology，股票代號：6667）成立於 1995 年，
深耕高科技製造供應鏈布局，並由既有廠務供應設備系統服務，
逐步拓展至機電統包、建廠總承攬及整合型工程解決方案。目前
業務範疇涵蓋廠務設備、製程二次配裝機服務，以及高科技廠房
整合建置與機電統包工程，可依客戶建廠及擴產需求，提供從工
程規劃、設計、採購、施工、測試驗證至系統整合與專案管理的
一站式服務。憑藉跨工種整合、精密製程系統建置及大型專案管
理能力，公司持續提升高附加價值工程案件比重，協助客戶縮短
建廠時程、確保工程品質並提升營運效率。因應全球淨零轉型與
資源循環需求，信紘科技亦積極發展製程機能水設備、廢液處理
與循環經濟解方、資源零廢中心建置等業務，將工程整合能力延
伸至綠色製造與永續基礎建設。

灣完整的高標準供應鏈以及敏捷的人力與材料調度，能高效支援
客戶新建、擴建及改建廠需求，加上工程需求穩定且執行週期較
短（主系統約 6 至 8 個月，二次配約 4 至 6 個月），構築了
公司長期穩健的營業基礎。

為打造未來成長新引擎，公司順應客戶海外建廠的全球布局與緊
湊時程，將成熟的工程能力輸出海外，信紘科不僅以既有產物工
程為切入點，逐步擴展至純水、廢水、無塵室及機電等領域以擴
大單一案件服務範疇，在非美日等新興區域更結合策略夥伴共同
承攬；同時，公司統包能力持續升級，從早期高技術門檻的二次
配工程，拓展至跨系統二次配統包，進而邁向承接大規模、高整
合度的跨國 GC 與工程管理角色，透過整合台外資源提升接單層
級與案件金額，全面貼近客戶全球建廠需求。

二.產業趨勢概況 
市場概況 
A.半導體產業概況  
半導體產業為全球數位經濟之核心基礎建設，近年在人工智慧
（AI）、高效能運算（HPC）及邊緣運算應用加速普及帶動下，產
業需求結構出現顯著變化。先進製程與高密度運算晶片需求持續
提升，使晶圓廠投資動能自庫存調整週期中快速復甦，並進入新
一輪資本支出擴張階段。 根據國際半導體產業協會（SEMI）2025
年發布之預測，全球 300mm 晶圓廠設備支出於 2026 年至 2028 年
間累計將達約 3,740 億美元，其中 2025 年單年度支出將首次突破
1,000 億美元水準。顯示全球半導體建廠投資已由短期循環性波
動，逐步轉為結構性成長態勢。另依 Deloitte（2025）預估，全球
半導體銷售額有機會於 2030 年前後突破 1 兆美元規模，反映產業
長期需求動能仍具成長潛力。

B.半導體產業 ESG 趨勢 
隨淨零排放與國際永續規範逐步落實，ESG 已由合規要求轉為半
導體供應鏈的重要競爭要素。依 SNS Insider（ 2025）預估，全球
半導體廢液處理市場將由 2024 年的 27.2 億美元成長至 2032 年的
49.2 億美元，其中亞太地區受惠於晶圓廠持續擴建與永續轉型，
成長動能可望高於全球平均。在此趨勢下，半導體廠務環保技術
逐步朝 「資源再生、能源轉化、源頭減量」三大方向發展。首先，
硫酸、氨氮廢水及有機溶劑等製程廢液，正由傳統末端處理轉向
回收再生，經純化後重新作為工業級或電子級原料使用，提高資
源循環率並降低廢棄物處置需求。其次，有機廢液處理逐步導入
Waste-to-Energy 技術，將廢液轉化為燃料或能源使用， 以降低外購
能源需求與碳排放。

此外，先進製程亦更加重視源頭減量，透過綠色化學品替代與先
進流體製程，降低強酸、強鹼及水資源使用量。隨 3nm 以下製程
持續發展，兼具降低化學品耗用、廢水負荷與營運成本效益的技
術，導入需求可望提升。 
整體而言，未來半導體廠務市場將由單純設備供應，逐步轉向整

Document 120: 9月美国就业数据点评：非农就业超预期走弱，年内加息概率仍较高

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月美国就业数据点评：非农就业超预期走弱，年内加息概率仍较高.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

证券研究报告
总量研究 / 宏观研究 2026 年 10 月 08 日
细分研究
非农就业超预期走弱，年内加息概率仍较高
——9 月美国就业数据点评
相关研究
-
投资要点：
 事件：10 月 2 日美国劳工部公布就业数据显示，9 月美国新增非农就业 2.9 万人，失业率为
4.2%。
 核心观点：9 月美国就业市场明显降温。（1）总体看，新增非农就业大幅回落，失业率小幅
上升。（2）结构看，制造业对就业支撑减弱，服务业不同行业表现分化。（3）工资看，薪资
增速继续放缓，就业市场对通胀支撑作用边际减弱。（4）政策预期看，非农就业走弱降低市
场对 10 月加息的预期，但通胀压力仍存，市场认为年内加息概率仍较高。
 总体：新增岗位明显回落，失业压力边际上升。
 美国就业增长再度放缓，劳动力市场降温趋势延续。（1）新增非农就业明显回落。9 月美国新
增非农就业 2.9 万人，较 8 月的 13.3 万人明显下降，显示新增招聘需求走弱。（2）失业率小
幅上升。9 月美国失业率上行 0.1 个百分点至 4.2%，印证劳动力市场边际走弱。总体而言，

9 月美国就业数据明显弱于前期，新增岗位大幅减少，失业率小幅上升，显示美国就业扩张动
能进一步减弱。
 结构：制造业支撑减弱，服务业表现分化。
 行业层面延续分化格局，制造业就业支撑有所减弱，服务业新增就业分化。（1）制造业新增
就业边际走弱。9 月制造业新增就业主要由耐用品制造业带动，但增量较上月明显回落；非耐
用品制造业就业基本持平，制造业整体扩张动能仍有限。（2）传统服务行业新增就业降温。
教育和保健服务、住宿和餐饮业新增就业较上月均有所回落，其中住宿和餐饮降幅较为明显，
服务业就业吸纳能力较上月减弱。（3）服务业新增就业整体减弱，但部分行业改善明显。贸
易、运输和公用事业就业保持较大增量，专业和商业专业技术服务延续扩张，艺术和娱乐业就
业由降转增，支撑服务业就业增长。总体看，制造业与服务业整体均转弱，美国劳动力市场整
体扩张趋弱。
 工资：薪资增速继续放缓，支撑通胀作用减弱。
 劳动力市场降温带动薪资增速放缓，就业对通胀支撑作用有所减弱。（1）薪资同比增速持续
回落。9 月美国私营非农部门平均时薪同比增长 3.0%，较上月进一步放缓，表明企业用工成

本上涨压力有所缓解。（2）薪资环比保持温和。9 月私营非农部门平均时薪环比涨幅为 0.1%，
较上月有所放缓，短期工资上涨压力相对有限。总体来看，就业需求走弱与薪资增速回落相互
印证，劳动力市场对服务价格和核心通胀的推动作用边际减弱。
 政策：就业走弱难改紧缩取向，年内加息仍有可能。
 就业市场降温削弱进一步收紧货币政策的必要性，但通胀仍对美联储政策形成约束。（1）就
业数据走弱压低短期加息预期。非农就业明显回落，失业率小幅上升，市场对美联储 10 月加
息预期有所降温。（2）年内加息预期仍较强。尽管就业市场边际走弱，市场仍认为美联储在
12 月议息会议上存在加息 25 个基点的可能，显示通胀压力仍是影响政策决策的重要变量。
（3）美联储对就业形势仍持相对乐观判断。美联储将全年失业率预测从上季度的 4.3%下调
至 4.1%，表明其对就业市场基本稳定仍具信心。总体看，就业数据虽走弱，但未改美联储短
期紧缩倾向。
 总结而言，9 月美国就业市场呈现需求放缓、结构分化、薪资降温的特征。新增就业明显减

少，失业率小幅上升，制造业支撑减弱，服务业表现分化，薪资增速持续放缓，显示就业市场
边际走弱。往后看，就业市场是否延续平稳降温及通胀能否回落，将继续共同影响美联储后
续政策节奏。
 风险提示：美国就业数据超预期，美国通胀水平超预期，地缘冲突风险。
证券分析师

报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为股票评级和行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告发布日后 6 个月内的相对市场表现，也即：
以报告发布日后的 6 个月内的公司股价（或行业指数）相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌幅作为基准。其中：A 股市场：沪深 300
指数（000300.SH）；新三板市场：三板成指（899001.CSI）（针对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（899002.CSI）（针对做市转让标的）；

持有 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在-5%～5%之间

卖出 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅小于-5%

### Page 1

ISSUE COMMENT 
삼성바이오로직스 
3조원 유상증자, 중장기 투자계획을 고려한 자금조달 
홍가혜  kahye0.hong@daishin.com 2026.08.31 
삼성바이오로직스 
(207940) 
투자의견  Buy 목표주가  2,000,000 현재주가(08.28) 1,486,000

 3조원 유상증자는 향후 2~3년 투자 집중과 15.4조원 규모의 중장기 투자계획을 고려한 자금조달 결정으로 판단 
 단기 희석 부담은 불가피하나, 향후 PolyPeptide의 수익성·FCF 개선과 신규 CAPA 가동률 상승 여부가 중요

3조원 규모 주주배정 유상증자 결정 
 8/28, 삼성바이오로직스는 약 3조원 규모의 주주배정 유상증자를 공시함. 신주 발행 규모는 227만주로 기존 발행주식 수의 4.9%
이며, 예정 발행가는 132.2만원(할인율 15%)임. 조달자금 중 약 2.7조원은 PolyPeptide Group 인수, 약 0.3조원은 제2바이오캠퍼
스 시설투자에 사용할 예정임.  
 지난 7월 PolyPeptide Group 인수 발표 당시 보유현금 및 차입을 주요 조달 방안으로 제시했던 점을 감안하면, 예상보다 큰 규모
의 유상증자 결정은 단기 투자심리에 부담으로 작용할 수 있음. 실제 공시 당일 주가는 6.78% 하락함.

예상보다 큰 증자 규모, 중장기 투자계획까지 고려 
 동사는 향후 2~3년 내 집중될 투자재원 확보를 이번 유상증자의 주요 배경으로 제시함. PolyPeptide 인수를 포함해 2034년까지 
계획된 주요 투자 규모는 총 15.4조원임. 
 1H26 말 기준 부채비율은 51.3%, 보유현금은 약 2.2조원으로 추가 차입 여력은 존재함. 다만 PolyPeptide 인수를 기존 현금과 
차입 중심으로 조달할 경우 인수 이후 현금은 약 0.5조원까지 감소할 것으로 예상됨. 따라서 이번 증자는 유동성 부족에 따른 불
가피한 자금조달이라기보다, 향후 대규모 투자를 위한 재무적 유연성을 확보하려는 선택으로 판단함.  
 이번 조달금액 중 2.7조원은 PolyPeptide 인수에 사용될 예정임. 이번 인수를 통해 자체 증설 대비 빠른 펩타이드 시장 진입과 기
존 항체 중심의 포트폴리오 확대, 양사 고객 기반을 활용한 cross-selling이 가능할 것으로 기대함. 나머지 약 0.3조원은 총 투자
비 약 1.9조원의 6공장 건설에 투입될 예정임. 6 공장은 180kL 규모로 2029년 완공 예정이며, 이외에도 제3바이오캠퍼스, 미국 
생산시설 및 ADC·DP 설비 등 추가 투자가 예정되어 있음.

단기 주주 부담은 불가피, 향후 투자성과가 관건  
 기존 발행주식 수의 4.9%에 해당하는 신주 발행에 따른 주당가치 희석 및 단기 수급 부담은 불가피함. 현재 추가적인 유상증자 
계획은 없는 것으로 파악되나, 향후 대규모 투자 집행을 감안하면 영업현금흐름 외 차입을 포함한 추가 자금조달 가능성은 열려 
있음. 향후에는 PolyPeptide의 수익성 및 FCF 개선과 기존 고객 기반을 활용한 시너지 창출, 6공장을 포함한 신규 CAPA의 선수
주 및 가동률 상승 여부가 중요할 것으로 판단됨.

Wired for growth
Initiate coverage with an Outperform rating and target price of NT$270
ACES is entering a two-engine growth phase. It is a key power cable supplier to
Bloom Energy as Bloom doubles its fuel-cell capacity, and it is expanding its high-
speed connector content from AI server daughterboards to ASIC motherboards, as

a high-speed cable leg comes on stream. We forecast a 72% EPS Cagr over 2026-
28, with OPM expanding from 7.4% in 2026 to 11.0% in 2027. Our TP of NT$270
is based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, and we initiate coverage at Outperform.
Bloom Energy the key earnings driver in 2027
ACES's multiyear partnership with Bloom locks in its share as Bloom plans to lift
capacity from 2GW in 2026 to 4GW/6GW in 2027/28, based on our checks. ACES
has shipped fuel-cell power cables to Bloom since 1Q26 and will add external DLO

high-voltage cables, with ASP of US$80-100k per set. We expect Bloom power
cables to be NT$5.8bn of sales and account for 28% of group revenue in 2027.
Power products need little incremental R&D and benefit from strong operating
leverage. We expect overall OPM to expand from 7.4% in 2026 to 11.0% in 2027.
High-speed products a new driver
Previously, ACES supplied only daughterboard connectors, at 10-20 units per rack.
On ASIC platforms, where ODMs have more say in supplier selection, ACES has

secured the opportunity to ship both motherboard and daughterboard connectors
(about 30 units per rack) and broadens its connector product offerings. With Gen6
connectors carrying around a 15% ASP premium over Gen5, and ACES as one of
only four vendors qualified by CSPs at Gen6, we forecast server revenue growth of
107%/74% YoY in 2026/27. The newly added Gen6 PCIe cable product, qualified
by two CSPs, began shipping in April 2026 at 20% of GM, with room to rise towards

30% as yield improves. Hence, we expect GM to recover to 26% in 2027.
Capacity in place ahead of demand
ACES has committed firm capacity ahead of demand. For power cables, it has leased
a new facility next to its Philippine plant, with new capacity planned to come online
in 4Q26. For server products, it plans to raise connector capacity from 4m to 5m
units/mo and will expand high-speed cable production lines from two to eight.
Initiate coverage with an Outperform rating

We forecast 2027/28SP EPS of NT$11.19/NT$14.93, with our 2027 forecast 48%
ahead of consensus. Our TP of NT$270 is based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, backed
by a 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-28SP driven by power cable ramp-up and high-speed
connector shipment growth. We initiate coverage with an Outperform rating.

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF

Bloom Energy the key earnings driver in 2027
We see tremendous advantages for ACES from Bloom Energy’s strong demand backed
by its aggressive expansion plan. ACES supplies inner power cables used inside each
fuel cell in 2026 and will begin supplying external diesel locomotive (DLO) cables that
connect fuel cell stacks into a fuel cell module (energy server). Each set (including inner
cable and external cable) carries dollar content of US$80-100k.

Furthermore, ACES has worked with Bloom on high-voltage power cables for SOFC
for around six years, even though the real contribution did not transpire until 2026. On
the back of this long-standing relationship, ACES is the key supplier during the
development phase and holds at least a 30% share in the mass production stage.
Together with these considerations, we expect inner cables to contribute revenue of
NT$5,113m/NT$8,074m in 2027/28, while external cables will add revenue of

NT$707m/NT$1,154m.
Figure 1
Power cable revenue from Bloom Energy and YoY change
Source: SinoPac
As power scarcity remains the key bottleneck for AI datacentre construction, Bloom
serves as a suitable solution and offers fuel cell systems that generate electricity from
natural gas. Its modular, on-site, 24/7 power platform stands apart in the power sector.
Against a conventional grid built on centralised generation, Bloom delivers compelling
time-to-power, cleaner air and water emissions profile, native-DC compatibility with

emerging 800V AI datacentre architectures, and a solution that communities and
nearby residents accept more readily.

As of year-end 2025, Bloom's product backlog grew about 140% YoY to roughly
US$6bn. Amid the surging demand, Bloom is improving backlog conversion and
stepping up capacity expansion to capture the strong growth opportunity. In 2Q26,
Bloom raised its full-year revenue guidance to US$3.9-4.2bn, up from US$3.4-3.8bn,
and plans to double annual capacity from about 1GW in 2025, split across its California

and Delaware sites, to 2GW by the end of 2026; and will further expand the capacity
to 4GW/6GW in 2027/28, based on our channel checks.

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF

infrastructure, with Bloom providing its fuel cell-based power generation systems and
Brookfield providing capital and infrastructure development capabilities. The strategic
financing framework expanded on June 30, 2026, from the original $5bn (October
2025) to $25bn, a fivefold increase. On customer wins, Nebius dropped its previously
planned gas and combustion turbines in May 2026 and switched to Bloom’s SOFC

solution, signing a 10-year master agreement worth up to $2.6bn. The first phase
covers 328MW of installed capacity, or roughly 250MW of guaranteed capacity, and
is expected to come online within 2026. Bloom also expanded its Oracle contract from
an initial 1.2GW to up to 2.8GW and signed deals including AEP (up to 1 GW) and
Equinix (over 100 MW across 19 datacentres), broadening its customer base materially.
Wide range of power cable products
ACES supplies Bloom with a variety of power cables across different specifications,

including Power Conversion System (PCS) cables, hot box cables and Diesel
Locomotive (DLO) high-voltage cables, rather than a single product type. In 2026,
ACES only supplies PCS cables and hot box cables, while the company will expand its
product offerings to DLO high-voltage cables from 2027, which we expect to
contribute another NT$707m of sales for the whole year.
The PCS cables and hot box cables are the inner power cables. Hot box cables
interconnect individual fuel cells within the hot box, where power generation creates

significant heat and therefore requires materials with higher durability and heat
resistance. Kaori and MTAR supply hot boxes for fuel cell power generation systems,
which require ACES’s hot box cables as one of their key components.
On the other hand, PCS cables are installed inside the Power Conversion System (PCS)
and are supplied to AcBel, a PCS solution provider. The PCS converts DC output into
usable power and manages load changes. As SOFCs generate DC power with voltage

varying according to load, the output cannot be directly connected to a datacentre.
The external power cable portfolio is mainly DLO high-voltage cables, which transmit
high-voltage DC power between fuel cell platforms, power modules and microgrid
systems. As external cables need to withstand higher voltage levels, their dollar content
is expected to be slightly higher than that of inner power cables.
Lastly, ACES also ships cables directly to Bloom Energy for development purposes.

DLO high-voltage cable opportunity heading into 800V DC era
We also see DLO cables, which are currently used outside the fuel cell, as having
significant potential after introducing 800V architecture. Under conventional AC
architecture, grid power, onsite turbines and reciprocating engines supply AC

power, and the downstream transformer, switchgear and UPS cables belong to the
utility/EPC supply chain, which ACES does not address. Bloom can deliver native
DC power directly to the rack, so the DC output cables might become part of
Bloom’s system. We see this opening as a new content opportunity for ACES as a
qualified high-voltage power cable supplier.
Within Bloom’s energy server under 800V architecture, the longer series stack of
fuel cells and the replacement of the inverter with DC-DC conversion require a

redesign of the internal harness to high-voltage specifications, which we believe
positions ACES to ship a growing volume of high-voltage DLO cables over time.

strong contribution from two major ASIC customers. Looking ahead, with the new
connector capacity coming online and increasing ASP driven by connector spec
upgrade, we expect server revenue to further grow 74%/33% in 2027/28SP.

Broadening connector footprint in ASICs
Since Nvidia has strict control over its supply chain, ACES previously supplied only
the daughterboard high-speed connectors for GB200/300 servers, at roughly 10 to
20 units per rack. On ASIC platforms, since ODMs have greater influence over
supplier selection, ACES secures share on both the motherboard and the
daughterboard, lifting content to around 30 units per rack.

Besides, ACES historically supplied only MCIO connectors to these ASIC customers,
but as further qualifications clear, the company will broaden its connector product
offerings in 2026 to include Multi-Track, board-to-board, and E1/E3 connectors.
We expect the contribution from ASIC customers to scale further in 2H26, driving
server revenue to grow 64% HoH.

Riding the Gen6 ASP curve with a compatibility advantage
Aside from the spectacular growth in the connector shipments, we expect ACES to
benefit from the ongoing Gen6 connector upgrade cycle, with ASPs expected to
increase by 15%. The AI server trays currently adopt a mix of Gen5 and Gen6
connectors, but we expect the penetration of Gen6 connectors to gradually
increase going forward as the migration from Gen5 to Gen6 is the first major

generational shift since Genoa introduced Gen5 in 2022.

Figure 15
Gen6 connector ASP is 15% higher than Gen5
Source: SinoPac
Only three to four connector vendors are qualified by CSPs at Gen6, and ACES is
one of them. Furthermore, ACES can supply both Gen5 and Gen6 platforms under
its existing qualification, with no need to re-sample or re-qualify for Gen5. This is
because Gen6 connectors keep footprint backward compatibility with Gen5. By
contrast, other peers must re-sample and run through the full qualification process

again to move up to Gen6.
About the PCIe migration and Gen6 spec upgrade
PCIe has long been used for on-board and in-chassis interconnects. In the Vera
Rubin platform, PCIe Gen6 serves as the in-tray bridge to front-of-tray modules
such as CX-9 SuperNICs, BlueField-4 DPUs and NVMe storage. GPU-to-GPU scale-
up runs on NVLink, with UALink as the open alternative; these fabrics work
alongside PCIe.
In order to address ever-larger training models, the PCIe must scale with the fast-

growing size of model parameters and datasets. The move from Gen5 to Gen6 is
not a simple speed doubling, even though per-lane bandwidth rises from 32 GT/s
to 64 GT/s. It reworks the link from the coding scheme up. Gen1 through Gen5
doubled I/O speed using NRZ signaling. Gen6 switches to PAM4 four-level
signaling and, for the first time, adds FEC (Forward Error Correction), which lets the
receiver detect and correct transmission errors on its own.

Each generation doubles bandwidth while tightening signal-integrity specs, which
demands higher-precision connector manufacturing. This creates a real barrier to
entry for high-speed connector suppliers.

High-speed cables emerging as a potential driver
ACES previously supplied connectors only. After several years of effort, its Gen6 PCIe
cable has now been qualified by two CSP clients, and the company began shipping the
in-rack cable product in April 2026. We expect high-speed cable shipments to enlarge
after the new capacity comes online and more CSP orders in 2H26. Hence, we expect
high-speed cable revenue to grow from NT$268m in 2026 to NT$793/NT$982m in

2027/28.
Currently, its high-speed cable product carries gross margin of merely 20%, but it is
likely to edge up to around 30% as the scale enlarges, and yield improves.

Capacity in place ahead of demand
ACES is rapidly expanding its high-speed cable and connector capacity, with high-
speed cable production lines expected to increase from two in 2025 to eight in
2H26, while it will proactively ramp up connector monthly capacity from 4m units
in July 2026 to 5m units by the end of 2026 to address customer demand ahead of
order placement.
Supported by strong customer demand and sufficient capacity, ACES expects total

connector shipments to reach 25m units in 2H26 and 50m units in 2027,
representing a significant increase from 15m units in 1H26.
As for power cables for Bloom Energy, ACES has leased a new facility next to its
Philippine plant, with the new power cable capacity planned to come online in
4Q26.

Diversified growth engines
ACES’s networking product portfolio includes set-top boxes, WiFi devices, routers,
and other networking equipment. We expect the business to benefit from
technology upgrades and equipment replacement driven by advances in wireless
communications, IoT, cloud computing, and cybersecurity. However, tariff and
inflationary pressures, together with component shortages among Asian customers,

are likely to limit growth. We expect networking product revenue to grow by 34%
and 14% in 2026 and 2027, respectively.

Automotive: rising electronic content
ACES’s product range spans ADAS, reversing radar and camera, body control, smart
cockpit, smart antenna, and high-voltage connectors and harnesses, extending into
applications such as EV charging guns. It has entered a US EV major as well as

European and Chinese Tier-1 supply chains. Going forward, it will continue to focus
on high-voltage, high-frequency, and high-speed connection technologies. The
company expects the automotive sector to stay flat in 2027 to reflect muted
demand and limited internal resource allocation, as it focuses on AI-related business
at this stage. In the long-term, growth from the automotive sector will be driven by
ADAS upgrades, which lifts demand for sensors and compute, alongside rising EV

penetration, which adds high-value electronic systems and power semiconductor
content.

Industrial: a steady UPS-driven base
ACES supplies industrial power cords, used mainly in uninterruptible power systems
(UPS). A UPS is an electrical device that acts as a reliable buffer when the main
power supply behaves abnormally, such as during a fault, outage, or unstable
voltage. It keeps automation controllers, sensors, and actuators continuously

powered so equipment keeps running. When a power problem occurs, the UPS
supplies stable backup power to prevent equipment failure, data corruption, or
system crashes.  We expect ACES’s industrial business to grow steadily, with growth
of 9% and 14% in 2026 and 2027, respectively.
Figure 29 Figure 30
ACES Industrial product portfolio Industrial sales and YoY
Source: ACES Source: SinoPac

easing component shortages and broader AI adoption, which should drive consumer
replacement demand, lifting global NB shipments -5%/5% year over year in
2027/28, which is a gradual recovery following a weaker 2026.

Power rack: leveraging its long-standing relationship with Schneider

ACES has a long-standing relationship with Schneider Electric at its Philippine plant.
The partnership dates back more than 20 years, with ACES’s Philippine plant
initially serving Schneider almost exclusively. Over the years, the product portfolio
has expanded from conventional power cables to uninterruptible power systems
(UPS) and, more recently, power racks for AI datacentres.
ACES began shipping high-voltage power cables for power racks in 1Q26 and

entered the volume ramp-up phase in 2Q26. We remain positive on power rack
adoption as the PSU in every IT tray hits a physical limit as rack power reaches
1MW, occupies rack space and caps compute density. Moving power shelves and
BBU/CBU into a dedicated power rack decouples power from computing and frees

Initiate with an Outperform rating
We initiate coverage of ACES with an Outperform rating given its dual growth
engine from Bloom’s strong expansion and high-speed connector customer wins
and generation upgrades. We forecast 2027/28 EPS of NT$11.19/NT$14.93, with
our 2027 forecast 48% ahead of consensus. Our TP of NT$270 is based on 20x
2H27-1H28SP PE, backed by a 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-28SP driven by Bloom’s

significant ramp and larger content in server products. Initiate coverage with an
Outperform rating.
Company profile
ACES was founded in 1996, and it mainly produces connectors, micro-coaxial cable,
high-speed transmission cables, and RF connectors. Connectors and cable
assemblies link two separate subsystems or operating units and carry the signals
between them, allowing the overall system to function properly. They are used
across a wide range of electronic products, with main applications spanning

notebooks and PC peripherals, consumer electronics, automotive electronics, cloud
servers, networking equipment, and industrial equipment.
To keep pace with the push towards lighter, thinner, and smaller notebooks and
consumer devices, ACES developed high-temperature-resistant, fine-pitch, and
low-profile products at an early stage. At the same time, it is moving actively into
the high-frequency, high-speed connectors, high-speed cables, and power supplies

required for cloud servers, as well as the connector and cable assemblies used in
automotive electronics.

Company development
ACES has used M&A well to gain new capabilities, plants and technology, which in
turn opened doors to new customers and markets. In 2015, it bought MEC IMEX
to add cable assembly capability and a plant in the Philippines, which took it into
the industrial market. In 2017, the CPC deal gave it access to the European auto
connector market and a plant in Vietnam. In 2019, it bought Kuang Ying to add

metal stamping capacity and reach Taiwan telecom customers. The key deal came
in 2021, when ACES bought 100% of Genesis, a US high-speed interconnect maker
based in Atlanta. The main goal was to gain Genesis’s long-standing ties with US
CSP and cloud customers. In 2025, ACES opened its Phase Two R&D headquarters
in Taoyuan, which focuses on cloud connectors and high-speed cables. We believe
10 years of M&A have turned ACES from a consumer connector maker into a

supplier of high-speed interconnect solutions for AI servers and the cloud.

Cashflow and balance sheet
ACES’s balance sheet shows healthy debt exposure with an improving net cash
level. Given its plan to massively expand capacity at its Taiwan plants, we expect
Capex to remain at a high level of NT$1,075m in 2026 and then slightly decline to
NT$855m in 2027. We forecast its depreciation-to-sales ratio to be around 3% in

2027, which is at the low end of its historical level.
We expect ACES’s ROE to expand from 10% in 2026 to 21% in 2027 and 23% in
2028, as the volume ramp of AI high-speed connectors and Bloom Energy power
cables drives a richer product mix and gross margin expansion, delivering 121% EPS
growth in 2027.
We expect ACES’s FCF to turn positive in 2026 as operating cashflow scales with
the AI high-speed connector and Bloom power cable ramp while Capex normalises

after the 2026 capacity buildout. Looking ahead, we expect ACES’s free cashflow
per share to be NT$0.8-1.5 in coming years.

Competitive positioning

In high-speed connectors, ACES competes primarily with overseas peers including
Amphenol (APH), TE, and Molex. APH’s edge lies in a development-technology lead of
one to two generations, while ACES’s relative strength rests on customers’ recognition
on delivering quality comparable to the overseas majors, alongside more flexible lead
times, strong capacity-expansion support, and fast ramp-up.
The structural opportunity comes from the supply chain integration trend. As

transmission speeds rise, CSPs increasingly prefer suppliers which can integrate high-
speed connectors with high-speed cables and ship assembled, finished goods.

### Page 29

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF

ACES is one of the few Taiwanese players with this vertical-integration footprint: it
holds wire-drawing technology transferred in-house from ITRI, produces everything
from wire drawing through assembly on its own, and its commitment to moving into
high-speed cables has been noted by AWS and Meta, backed by roughly two years of
qualification work already completed.
By contrast, other Taiwanese peers have yet to build comparable vertical integration.

Customers have historically split connectors and cables across separate suppliers, with
cable suppliers represented by BizLink (3665 TT) and Longwell (6290 TT), while peers
such as Lotes (3533 TT), Alltop (3526 TT), Argosy (3217 TT) and JPC Connectivity
(6197 TT) are likewise strong in connectors but lack in-house cable capability.
Since the Gen5 qualified-supplier base was already fragmented, the Gen6 bar of
combined connector-plus-cable capability becomes ACES’s entry point, bringing ACES

into design-in from Gen6. The qualified connector supplier base is small, with suppliers
including APH, TE, and ACES, with Lotes added subsequently.
Peer comparison
ACES’s primary local competitors in connectors include Lotes, Alltop, Argosy and JPC
Connectivity. In the cable industry, it competes with Bizlink and Longwell. ACES’s
foreign peers include Amphenol, TE and FIT.

Target price calculation
We initiate coverage of ACES with an O-PF rating and a target price of NT$270,
based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, supported by a strong 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-

28SP. We use a PE-based methodology for ACES, in line with other connector
companies. We initiate coverage with an Outperform rating.
Our target multiple of 20x is in line with the 2027SP average PE of 20.4x for Taiwan-
listed connector and cable peers with direct AI datacentre exposure, namely
BizLink, Lotes and JPC Connectivity. We view this as a reasonable anchor. ACES
offers faster earnings growth than the group, with 2027SP EPS growth of 121%

versus the peer average of 52%, while its 2027-28SP ROE of 21-24% sits at the low
end of the peer range of 22-52%.

We also believe the target PE premium is justified as the AI revenue contribution,
including AI data transmission and power, rises from 4% in 2024 to 48% in 2027SP.
The company maintains its key supplier position at Bloom as the company ramps up
its capacity expansion, while ASIC customer wins and higher connector content
from the Gen6 upgrade cycle should provide additional growth drivers. As the
company gradually shifts away from its legacy consumer electronics base and its

exposure to cyclical downturns, we see room for a structural rerating.

Key risks
We see four key operational risks for ACES:
Raw material cost volatility: Copper prices drive a large share of Cogs for ACES's
cable products, while connectors carry far less exposure. ACES currently reprices
its cable products with customers on a quarterly basis, which has allowed it to

sustain a long-term GM of around 20% on power cables. However, the pass-through
might be lagging, so sharp copper price swings could cause quarterly GM to
fluctuate before repricing impact catches up.
Delays in ODM pull-in: ACES ships its high-speed connectors directly to ODM
customers. Because rack assembly sits at the final stage of the AI server supply
chain, any upstream disruption, such as components shortages and assembly yield
issue, could push out rack shipment schedules or reduce volumes. This could in turn

lead ODMs to cut or defer orders for high-speed connectors from ACES, creating
quarter-to-quarter volatility in server revenue.
Slower-than-expected ramp-up at Bloom Energy: Power cables for Bloom will
account for 28%/35% of ACES's revenue in 2027/28SP, which makes Bloom's
ramp-up the single largest swing factor in our forecasts. Delays could arise on both
the supply and demand sides. On the supply side, Bloom could fall behind its
capacity plan if new facility fit-out, equipment installation or hiring takes longer

than expected. On the demand side, its datacentre customers could defer
deployments due to grid interconnection, gas supply or financing hurdles, or due to
regulatory headwinds such as tighter permitting and emissions rules for on-site gas-
fired generation, utility restrictions on behind-the-meter power, or changes to fuel
cell tax incentives. Either scenario would directly reduce ACES's power cable
shipments. As volumes scale, Bloom may also qualify a second cable supplier, which

would add pressure to ACES's share and ASP.
Prolonged NB weakness: NB and Consumer remains ACES's largest segment at 35%
of 2026SP revenue, and our forecasts assume it returns to 4%/12% growth in
2027/28SP on a Windows 12-driven replacement cycle. If end-demand stays soft
or the replacement cycle slips, a further NB decline could offset part of the growth
from Bloom and AI servers and dilute group GM.

### Page 31

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 31
Investment thesis
ACES is evolving from a notebook connector maker into a supplier of AI
interconnects and power cables, with two growth engines ramping at the same
time. The first is Bloom Energy. ACES has co-developed SOFC power cables with
Bloom for about six years, and it is currently one of three suppliers of Bloom's power
cable assemblies, with around one-third of the total. It ships inner PCS and hot-box

cables in 2026 and adds external DLO high-voltage cables from 2027, with order
visibility extending to 2028 and beyond. The second engine is AI/ASIC servers. On
ASIC platforms, ODMs have more say in supplier selection, so ACES wins both
motherboard and daughterboard sockets, which lifts content from 10-20 to ~30
units per rack. Gen6 raises connector ASP by ~15%, and a new in-chassis PCIe Gen6
cable line, qualified by two CSPs, adds a higher-content stream. ACES integrates

vertically from wire drawing to final assembly, which makes it one of the few
Taiwanese suppliers able to ship connector-plus-cable solutions as CSPs
consolidate their vendor base.
Catalysts
(1) Bloom's capacity ramp from 2GW to 4GW/6GW in 2026-28 and new data-
center wins (Oracle, Nebius, AEP, Equinix) converting into cable orders; (2) DLO
high-voltage cable shipments starting in 2027; (3) ASIC customer ramps in 2H26
and a broader product range beyond MCIO into Multi-Track, board-to-board and

E1/E3 connectors; (4) rising Gen6 penetration on AI server trays; (5) high-speed
cable lines expanding from two to eight by end-2026, with GM improving from
~20% toward ~30%.
Valuation details
We adopt a PE-based valuation methodology as we believe the market will mainly
focus on the earnings growth outlooks of high-speed connector makers, which is
better reflected with PE metrics. We assign a target multiple of 20x PE, more than
+1sd of the three-year average, to reflect a strong outlook with margin expansion

and an EPS Cagr of 72% in 2026-28 on solid Bloom Energy demand and increasing
high speed connector content.
Investment risks
There could be downside risk to sales growth and margins if: (1) Bloom's
deployments or backlog conversion slow on financing, gas supply or permitting
delays, or ACES loses share among Bloom's cable suppliers; (2) ASIC programs ramp
more slowly or CSP Capex is cut; (3) Gen6 adoption is slower than expected, or
competition from Amphenol, TE, Molex and Lotes pressures ASP; (4) high-speed

cable yield and GM improve more slowly than we assume; (5) NB and consumer
demand weakens further on component shortages, tariffs and inflation; (6) TWD
appreciation or higher copper prices squeeze margins.

Document 247: FX Daily_ Hot inflation to strengthen dollar floor

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Daily_ Hot inflation to strengthen dollar floor.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

We expect MoM US CPI to be 0.1pp higher than consensus in
both headline (0.7%) and core (0.3%) today. While that
shouldn’t be enough for markets to price in 50%+ chances of
an October Fed hike, it can reinforce expectations of further
tightening down the road and, by extension, the dollar’s ﬂoor.
EUR/USD may retest recent lows before any recovery

Frantisek Taborsky, Francesco Pesole and Chris Turner
We expect month-on-month US CPI to be 0.1pp higher than consensus in both
headline and core today
We have published the October edition of FX Talking: Au
revoir, euro bulls, with updated FX forecasts and views.
USD: Upside risks persist
The dollar had a soft session yesterday as stretched positioning
and short-term overvaluation coincided with a recovery in global
bonds. A dovish comment by the Fed’s John Williams added a bit of

### Page 2

weight to the dollar, more by supporting risk sentiment than
triggering much dovish repricing in the front end.
Williams said he still expects another hike this year, but there are
signs that inﬂation will slow and he doesn’t see further tightening in
2027. That is in line with the median Dot Plot, and Williams is a
dovish-leaning voice, but such an explicit pushback against market
expectations (three more hikes by June) has been uncommon.
Today, all eyes will be on US inﬂation data for September. The

consensus is for a 0.6% month-on-month acceleration in headline
CPI and 0.2% MoM in core. Our macro team is expecting slightly
hotter ﬁgures: 0.7% and 0.3% MoM, respectively.
For this reason, we expect some dollar re-strengthening today.
Markets may marginally increase expectations for an October hike,
but for pricing to return above 50% (now 20%) we probably need to
see headline close to 1.0% MoM and/or core at 0.4% MoM today.
Francesco Pesole
EUR: French premium getting entrenched

EUR/USD has climbed back to the 1.120 handle, entirely due to a
softer dollar. The euro is not really recovering momentum in other
crosses, a sign that French risk continues to weigh. The France-
Germany 10yr yield spread tightened around 5bp yesterday to
137bp, seemingly driven by relief in the global bond market rather
than idiosyncratic factors.
We have a one-month target of 1.13 in EUR/USD, but we could still
re-test the 1.1150-1.1160 area in the coming days. As discussed in

the USD section above, we see upside risks for USD today as US CPI
is released.
Francesco Pesole
SEK: Looking healthier
EUR/SEK has corrected around 1.8% from the 1 October 11.36 high,
with a major contribution from French ﬁscal risk. It’s important to
note EUR/SEK was starting from a marked (around 2%) short-term

overvaluation at the end of September, and it’s now fairly valued –
unlike EUR/USD, which is cheap according to our models.
The move is likely welcomed by the Riksbank, which has been
keeping a closer eye on the krona’s weakness now that inﬂationary
risks are rising. September CPIF inﬂation didn’t scream a rate hike,
headline at 1.5% and core at 0.5%, but the Riksbank has clearly
signalled it’s ready to hike in November, and we see a high bar for a

rethink.
A hawkish Riksbank and dovish repricing of ECB expectations have
narrowed the two-year EUR/SEK swap spread by nearly 20bp over
the past couple of weeks. While its correlation with EUR/SEK can be
patchy, the move currently supports a lower spot. Our one-month
target is 11.15, with risks tilted to the downside.
Francesco Pesole
CEE: Gains likely to be short-lived
Yesterday’s inﬂation data from the Czech Republic conﬁrmed a rise
in core inﬂation from 3.0% to 3.1%. Although this is an increase of

only one-tenth of a percentage point, it marks the highest ﬁgure
since December 2023. More importantly, the underlying structure
and accelerating momentum eﬀectively cement the case for a CNB
rate hike in November – barring a signiﬁcant improvement in the
geopolitical situation.
Speaking of inﬂation, a new report we released yesterday discussed
emerging inﬂationary risks for the CEE region stemming from
energy and food prices. Until now, food prices have oﬀset higher

fuel costs in the region, keeping headline inﬂation in check. This
dynamic is expected to shift in the coming months as we see a
lagged pass-through of global prices into local inﬂation.
Market sentiment yesterday was mixed, reﬂecting a catch-up
following Monday’s US close alongside natural gas prices hitting
new highs. The latter, in particular, suggests that the CEE region
cannot enjoy a respite for long. While we saw a rally in FX and rates
yesterday, we do not believe the rates rally is justiﬁed at this

juncture, given the rising risk of higher inﬂation and the strong

► 美股表現相對強勁 ，主要受科技 與半導體上
漲帶動 。其中標普 500與那斯達克指數一度
創高，費半指數漲勢則更為顯著。反映9月非
農數據放緩推升 10月聯準會按兵不動預期所
帶來的激勵效果延續 。另一方面 ，歐股則是
不漲反跌 ，因傳出法國債務風險疑慮與政局
不穩等 消息，帶動法國股市下跌 。日股在半
導體與金融聯袂反彈下，推動指數一度收復7

萬點整數關卡 ，港股則在陸股國慶假期休市
量能縮減下，指數亦表現疲弱。
► 產業 方面 ，馬斯 克 稱正與 台 積電 討 論 參與
Terafab 德州晶片計畫 ，加上超微表示將於
2027年大幅增加晶片供應 ，以因應AI產品需
求快速成長 ，執行長蘇姿 丰更赴 台與供應鏈
夥伴討論擴充 CPU與GPU產能，顯示AI半導
體長期發展趨勢強勁 ，激勵科技相關類股表
現。此外，G7釋放緊急原油與柴油庫存後 ，
減輕市場對能源價格與通膨的部分 擔憂，亦
帶動非必需消費產業上漲。

► 美國9月非農數據公布後一度令 10年期公債
殖利率出現明顯回落 。然觀察最新 ISM服務
業之價 格分 項 指 數 較上月 出現反彈 ， 並創
2022年7月以來 最高水準 ，反映運輸 及原物
料成本上升 ，顯示通膨壓力仍可能持續向其
他產業傳導 ，公債殖利率因而再度反彈並持
續於高檔徘迴 ，多數債券市場在利率高檔震
盪環境下亦呈現跌多漲少現象。
► 受法國債務問題與西班牙提前大選等歐洲 政
治與經濟消息影響 ，歐元走勢疲弱 。儘管近
期日圓於日銀9月升息後不升反貶，然市場亦
擔憂持續走貶可能再度引發干預 ，令短線日
圓呈現區間震盪走勢。
► G7宣布透過國際能源總署協調釋放 1億桶柴
油及原油等緊急 儲備，令油價短線回落 ，然
隨中東衝突前景未明 ，供應不穩之變數短期
難解，多空因素交錯恐使油價於高檔進行反
覆波動。

► 美國經濟仍具韌性 ，惟通膨壓力猶存 。觀察
美國9月ISM服務業指數表現 ，內需活動維持
穩健，企業投入成本壓力亦有所升溫；另一
方面，受地緣政治因素影響 ，美國柴油價格
大幅攀升 ，而柴油為卡車運輸 、農業及製造
業的重要燃料 ，若價格持續維持高檔 ，恐進
一步增添通膨壓力 。考量聯準會偏鷹立場 ，
且當前通膨降溫進程不如預期 ，年底前仍保
有升息空間。
► 美國債務總額突破40兆美元，預估2026年公
眾持有聯邦債務占 GDP的比率將達 101%，
財政可持續性風險升溫 。與此同時 ，AI基礎

建設舉債熱潮 ，疊加中東局勢動盪帶來的通
膨不確定性 ，迫使投資人重新定價長天期債
券風險 ，要求更高的風險溢酬 。隨實質殖利
率上行，美債10年期殖利率揚升並持續高懸
於5%水準之上，30年期長債殖利率亦同步站
上近20年來高點 。隨長天期債券利率續揚 ，
殖利率曲線趨陡峭 ，債券配置宜先避開長天
期部位，聚焦中短天期債券鎖利機會。

6
投資焦點
► 距離美國期中選舉投票僅剩不到1個月，觀察
Polymarket賭盤預估數據顯示 ，眾議院選情
較為明朗，民主黨有超過9成的機率控制眾議
院。但參議院選情則尚有變數 ，今年以來兩
黨均互有領先 ，雖然數據顯示民主黨掌控參
議院機率呈現上揚 ，然隨時序進入選前最後
衝刺期 ，參議院能否亦由民主黨掌控仍待觀
察。故就選前而言 ，由於不同政黨的政策傾
向差異 ，潛在受惠產業亦有不同 ，選舉結果
的不確定性恐令投資人不易大舉押注股市。
► 另一方面 ，油價高檔徘迴 ，導致年底前通 膨
擔憂仍存 ，加上AI企業發債需求成長 、美國
財政體質惡化等 因素共同發酵 ，推動近期 美
債殖利率逐步攀高 ，並令股市預期本益 比壓
力延續 ，進而限縮了投資人短期對美股的追
價動能，短期指數恐維持震盪輪動格局 。可
留意抗波動 、體質佳且具價值優勢之標的 ，
待期中選舉結束與3Q26財報展開後，不確定
性減退將有助資金回歸長線AI題材。
殖利率攀升令美股本益比面臨壓力參議院期中選舉選情仍具變數
期中選前擾動與殖利率走高，美股短期恐仍震盪輪動

### Page 7

7
投資焦點日股
► 9月日本銀行決議升息 1碼，明確表示潛在
CPI已接近2%，未來也將繼續調高利率 ，政
策重點開始轉向防範通膨向上偏離目標 。然
因有2位委員反對升息，加上總裁植田和男會
後談話偏向中性 ，令市場解讀此次升息未如
預期鷹派，美日利差不易快速縮窄，故9月會
議後日元反而呈現利多出盡的回貶反應。
► 觀察今年以來日股面臨短線日圓升值的狀態
並未出現顯著走弱 態勢。一方面反映企業治
理改革推動股東報酬 提升已成為 支撐股價的
重要結構性因素 。另一方面 ，升息代表日本
經濟已具備一定的健康程度 ，貨幣政策正常
化將不致對需求帶來衝擊 。因此 ，隨BoJ延
續升息方向 ，將擴大銀行淨利差 ，改善其獲
利結構，並提升金融資產收益 。儘管近期整
體日股預期獲利成長走勢略顯停滯 ，然日本
銀行股 在總 體經 濟 與貨幣 政策環 境的 支持
下，獲利成長有望逆勢攀升 ，料將成為後續
資金關注重點。
日圓升值對日股影響似乎有限，關注銀行股獲利走強BoJ升息方向延續，惟其步伐仍取決於經濟與物價

BoJ政策保持正常化路線，日本銀行股有望持續受惠

### Page 8

8
股市
 公債殖利率走高雖可能壓抑 股債市場近期 動能，然而觀察美股第三季獲利年增 率有望仍為雙位數成
長，或支撐股市表現 。市場持續關注AI資本支出是否帶來獲利成長 ，短期內評價面壓力將增添市場
動盪，可分散配置於不同國家與產業 ，降低市場波動影響 。總結上述，地緣衝突、殖利率偏高及評
價面壓力等紛擾 帶來干擾，可布局防禦 股增強抗震力 。長期而言，科技及AI半導體發展趨勢不變且
基本面佳，可逢拉回時分批布局。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，加上全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍
工仍有投資題材。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融及醫療保健產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，且最新聯準會利率點陣圖顯示 ，官員預估今年底前仍有一碼升息空間 ，立場
偏向維持較高利率水平抑制通膨 發展。殖利率偏高環境建議以中短天期債券投資為主 ，可逢利率走
升時布局進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸

引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期的鷹派，日圓震盪。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪
商品
 中東地緣衝突短期紛擾難解，偏高的油價可能對通膨及實體經濟帶來影響。
 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法
類別 市場看法 偏好資產
策略思維
策略思維 短期抗波動，逢回可分批布局仍具長線趨勢之AI與科技產業

### Page 15

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

EARNINGS AGGREGATES
ANALYST: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com;
The S&P 500 Earnings Scorecard is the most comprehensive and up to the minute compilation of aggregated earnings
growth and market intelligence covering the companies included in the S&P 500 Index.
This report combines I/B/E/S's unrivaled historical earnings database, in depth coverage of Wall Street analysts'
bottom-up corporate earnings estimates, and the analytic capabilities of the Proprietary Research group

and desk-top solutions.

Document 284: Nuclear energy_ One industry, many reactors Our latest primer explores the reactor technologies and hurdles shaping nuclear’s next chapter. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Nuclear energy_ One industry, many reactors Our latest primer explores the reactor technologies and hurdles shaping nuclear’s next chapter. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-09-01
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

13022014     1

Sustainability 
Nuclear energy: One industry, many reactors 
29 September 2026

Key takeaways 
• As power demand grows, nuclear has returned to the forefront of the energy conversation. Yet the industry is far from one-size-
fits-all, encompassing a range of reactor technologies designed to serve different markets, applications and deployment models. 
• Nuclear power is evolving beyond traditional large-scale reactors. While proven designs continue to anchor utility-scale 
generation, small modular reactors (SMRs) and microreactors are being developed to serve a broader range of applications 
through more flexible, modular deployment models, though most remain in the demonstration phase today. 
• The future of nuclear deployment will depend on more than reactor design alone. Supply chains, skilled labor, fuel availability and 
regulatory approvals remain critical challenges, though recent policy initiatives are intended to support licensing, fuel production 
and broader industry growth.

Nuclear energy has re-emerged at the center of the power conversation, driven by rising electricity demand and tightening 
supply-demand dynamics across the grid. Yet nuclear is not a single technology. It encompasses a wide range of reactor designs 
and sizes, each with distinct economics, risks and deployment pathways. Building off our previous nuclear publication (which can 
be found here: Nuclear energy: Who, what, where, when, why?), this primer unpacks that complexity, grounding the broader 
nuclear narrative in the practical realities of development, from technology and construction to fuel, licensing and execution.  
Reactor sizes 
Nuclear power is best understood as a collection of reactor classes differentiated by scale, deployment model and end use 
(Exhibit 1). Rather than competing directly with one another, these technologies are designed to serve different segments of the 
power market and, in many cases, can scale alongside one another. 
Exhibit 1: Different reactor types have different advantages and risks 
Comparison of reactor technologies

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
I NSTI TUTE
Accessible version

### Page 2

2 29 September 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Key Distinctions  
• Large reactors (≥1 gigawatt (GW)): Designed for large-scale electricity generation, these reactors provide consistent, high-
capacity power and serve as core assets within the grid. 
• Small modular reactors (SMRs)(~50–300 megawatts (MW)): Mid-scale systems designed for modular deployment across 
utilities, industrial users and large commercial customers, offering greater flexibility in siting and capacity expansion.  
• Microreactors (≤20 MW): Small, highly distributed systems intended for niche or off-grid applications where reliability, 
autonomy or energy security are primary considerations. 
Technology 
Nuclear power generation is based on a common principle: heat produced from nuclear fission is used to generate steam, which 
drives a turbine to create electricity (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: In a nuclear reactor, heat is formed by fission 
Process for a nuclear fission reaction

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Nuclear reactor designs can be grouped based on coolant type, neutron spectrum and fuel configuration. Most reactors currently 
operating in the US are light-water reactors (LWRs), though a wide range of advanced designs are being developed (Exhibit 3).  
Exhibit 3: Gen III & Gen IV+ technologies have different characteristics 
Reactor technologies 
Reactor Type Coolant Moderator Neutron Spectrum Operating Temperature Pressure 
Pressurized Water Reactor (PWR) Water Light water Thermal ~300–330°C High (~150 bar) 
Boiling Water Reactor (BWR) Water Light water Thermal ~280–290°C High (lower than PWR) 
Heavy Water Reactor (HWR) Heavy water Heavy water Thermal ~300°C High 
Molten Salt Reactor (MSR) Molten salt Graphite (typical) Thermal / fast (design-dependent) ~600–750°C Low 
High-Temperature Gas Reactor (HTGR) Helium gas Graphite Thermal ~700–800°C Low 
Sodium Fast Reactor (SFR) Liquid sodium None Fast ~450–550°C Low 
Gas-Cooled Fast Reactor (GFR) Helium gas None Fast ~700–850°C Low 
Source: BofA Global Research, Energy Information Administration (EIA), Idaho National Laboratory (INL), Nuclear Regulatory Commission (NRC) 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
AP1000 
The AP1000 is a Generation III+ pressurized water reactor with a capacity of approximately 1.1 GW per unit (Exhibit 4). Its design 
is centered around passive safety systems that rely on gravity, natural circulation and compressed gas, rather than large numbers 
of active pumps and diesel-backed safety systems. By reducing the number of safety-critical moving parts, this approach lowers 
system complexity and supports a more standardized large-reactor platform.

### Page 3

29 September 2026    3

I NSTI TUTE

Exhibit 4: AP1000s typically need about 1,000 acres of land 
Diagram of an AP1000 nuclear plant

Source: BofA Global Research, Nuclear Regulatory Commission (NRC) 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
The AP1000’s design emphasizes open-top construction, with large structural and equipment modules manufactured offsite and 
then assembled at the project site. This approach is intended to reduce onsite labor needs, improve quality control through 
factory fabrication and support a more repeatable, standardized construction process. 
The AP1000 is no longer a conceptual technology. It is a licensed, utility-scale nuclear design already operating in the field. As a 
result, the key remaining considerations center on construction costs, project execution and the economics of deploying 
additional units, according to BofA Global Research. 
SMRs 
Small modular reactors (SMRs) are nuclear power plants generally 300 MWe or less per unit and are designed around a factory-
based, modular construction model (Exhibit 5). Unlike traditional gigawatt-scale nuclear projects, SMRs represent a shift toward 
repeatable, standardized deployment (read our prior SMR publication: Q&A quick guide: Small modular reactors). Major 
components can be manufactured in a factory setting and then transported to project sites for assembly, reducing the amount of 
onsite construction required. Their standardized designs also create opportunities for learning-by-doing, potentially lowering 
costs over time. In addition, capacity can be added incrementally through discrete modules, allowing capital to be deployed in 
stages rather than committed upfront.

### Page 4

4 29 September 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Exhibit 5: SMRs typically require 50-200 acres of land, depending on design and location 
Diagram of an SMR

Source: BofA Global Research, Nuclear Regulatory Commission (NRC) 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Market landscape  
There are dozens of SMR designs globally, with more than 100 projects at various stages of development, though only a limited 
number are currently operational or under construction. While developers are pursuing a range of reactor technologies, light-
water reactor (LWR) designs still dominate the US development landscape (Exhibit 6). According to BofA Global Research, the 
sector remains largely in a development and demonstration phase rather than broad commercial deployment. 
Exhibit 6: LWRs make up 50% of the SMR designs 
US SMR designs by technology type (%)

Source: Energy Information Administration (EIA), BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

50%
SFR
19%

12%

### Page 5

29 September 2026    5

I NSTI TUTE

Microreactors 
Microreactors are nuclear power systems typically 20 MWe or less per unit, representing the smallest class of advanced reactors 
(Exhibit 7). They emphasize flexibility, allowing deployment where traditional grid-scale infrastructure is impractical. Many 
designs incorporate multi-year fuel cycles that reduce refueling requirements and associated logistics burdens, making them 
particularly attractive for remote communities, industrial sites and defense-related applications. 
Exhibit 7: Microreactors need about one to five acres of land depending on the design 
Diagram of a microreactor

Source: BofA Global Research, Nuclear Regulatory Commission (NRC) 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Market landscape  
A growing number of developers are pursuing microreactor technologies in the US, however, despite increasing interest, most 
projects remain in the demonstration and pilot phase rather than commercial deployment. According to BofA Global Research, 
federal government programs continue to play a central role in funding and advancing early deployments, with several initial 
projects targeted for later this decade. 
Supply chain 
Large reactors depend on specialized components and complex construction processes, limiting the number of qualified 
suppliers. Modular designs, on the other hand, shift a greater share of manufacturing from construction sites to factory 
environments, with the goal of improving consistency, reducing onsite complexity and enabling more standardized deployment. 
As reactor sizes decrease, component requirements generally become less specialized, expanding the potential supplier base and 
simplifying project execution. 
Workforce availability and quality assurance remain critical across all reactor types. Large projects require significant numbers of 
skilled tradespeople, including welders, electricians and nuclear-certified inspectors. The US nuclear workforce has declined over 
prior decades, leading to labor shortages during first-of-a-kind builds.  
Fuel 
Fuel is one of the most important building blocks in the advanced nuclear ecosystem, as availability, manufacturing capacity and 
regulatory approval are all critical to deployment. Because many next-generation reactors require specialized fuels, the

### Page 6

6 29 September 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

development of commercial-scale fuel supply chains will be a key factor in determining how quickly advanced nuclear 
technologies can be brought to market. 
HALEU 
High-assay low-enriched uranium (HALEU) refers to uranium enriched above the roughly 5% U-235 content used in conventional 
reactors but below the 20% threshold for highly-enriched uranium. HALEU enables smaller reactor cores and longer fuel cycles, 
making it a key requirement for many advanced reactor designs. 
Historically, commercial HALEU supply has been limited. To address this gap, the Department of Energy launched the HALEU 
Availability Program in 2024 to support enrichment, fuel processing and deconversion capacity. The government is also working 
to recover HALEU from existing materials, with a targeted recovery of roughly 10 to 26 metric tons over time. 
The first US HALEU production in decades occurred in 2023, though output remains limited. Current production is approximately 
900 kilograms annually and is government-owned, with material allocated primarily to research, fuel qualification and early 
demonstration projects. 
TRISO 
Tri-structural isotropic particle fuel (TRISO) is key for high-temperature gas-cooled reactors (HTGRs) and certain microreactors. 
Unlike conventional nuclear fuel, it requires specialized manufacturing and therefore is currently only limited to pilot-scale 
production. But key development efforts include DOE-led research through the Advanced Gas Reactor (AGR) program and 
expansion of private-sector manufacturing with DOE support. 
Regulatory approval is also required for new fuel designs, manufacturing processes and transportation systems. As a result, 
scaling TRISO production remains an important step toward the broader deployment of advanced reactors. 
Fuel Risk 
Fuel availability is a key execution risk for advanced nuclear projects. Many next-generation reactor designs rely on HALEU or 
TRISO fuel, both of which remain supply-constrained. As a result, reactor technology may be ready before fuel supply is, creating 
a potential bottleneck for deployment.  
Challenges include multi-year lead times for fuel production and certification, potential gaps between reactor licensing and fuel 
readiness, and the limited supplier base and transportation requirements associated with HALEU. Together, these factors can 
add uncertainty to project timelines and costs until fuel supply chains reach commercial scale. 
Licensing 
Licensing is a key determinant of nuclear project timelines, capital deployment and execution risk. Regulatory requirements 
shape not only whether a project is approved, but also how predictable construction schedules are and how easily reactor 
designs can be replicated. In the US, commercial reactors are licensed by the Nuclear Regulatory Commission (NRC), whose 
framework emphasizes safety reviews, environmental assessments and public participation. While this approach provides 
regulatory certainty, it can also create lengthy approval timelines. 
Large light-water reactors benefit from well-established regulatory processes, while frameworks for advanced reactors continue 
to evolve. For first-of-a-kind projects, licensing can extend four to eight years or longer depending on technology maturity, 
application quality, public intervention and regulatory resources.  
Policy  
Recent years have seen a flurry of initiatives to revitalize nuclear energy. A series of coordinated policy moves, demonstration 
programs, fuel supply efforts and industry partnerships have converged to create the conditions for a potential nuclear 
resurgence:  
• ADVANCE Act: Signed into law in July 2024, the ADVANCE (Accelerating Deployment of Versatile, Advanced Nuclear for 
Clean Energy) Act modernizes the NRC licensing process (making it more affordable and predictable). The legislation also  
facilitates the siting of new reactors at brownfield sites, supports domestic fuel-cycle capabilities, and includes broader NRC 
institutional reforms, such as workforce recruitment and retention initiatives, updates to the agency’s mission statement and 
provisions related to international nuclear cooperation and exports. 
• Executive orders: On May 23, 2025, the Trump administration issued four executive orders aimed at accelerating US nuclear 
deployment through licensing reform, expedited reactor testing, expanded national security applications and efforts to 
strengthen the domestic nuclear fuel and supply chain base. Since their issuance, federal agencies, particularly the 
Department of Energy, have taken steps to implement these directives. While progress has been made on several fronts, a 
number of the orders' longer-term objectives remain under active implementation, according to BofA Global Public Policy.

Document 353: Will earnings resolve the bond-equity split_

### Page 1

WS Global CIO Office 
9 October 2026

Will earnings resolve 
the bond-equity split?

 Bond and equity market volatility has 
sharply diverged. Elevated bond 
volatility and political risks in the US 
and Europe warrant near-term caution,

Charts of the week: Diverging volatility

Strong earnings should resolve the bond and equity market divergence; French political risks remain largely contained
